# Third-party notices

Slicer Project Manager is distributed under the MIT licence in `LICENSE` (repository root; the
desktop installer puts it beside the program as `third-party/LICENSE`). The programs also contain
software written by other people under other licences, and this file is the notice those licences
ask for. It is a statement about files, not legal advice.

This file is **generated** by `pnpm notices` (`tools/generate-notices.mjs`) from the lock files and
the licence files of the packages as they are installed, plus the hand-written sections in
`tools/notices-static.md`. Edit those, not this file.

## What ships

- **`spm-server`**, the server binary, and the Docker image built from the `Dockerfile`. The image is
  `FROM scratch` and holds one statically linked executable (Rust, linked against musl libc, with
  the web bundle embedded in it) and two empty directories. No operating-system packages, no shell
  and no other software are in it. Its contents are therefore: this application's own code, the Rust
  crates and the npm packages listed below, the Rust standard library, SQLite and musl libc.
- **The desktop installer** (`Slicer Project Manager_<version>_x64-setup.exe`, NSIS, per user). The
  program inside is the same server and web bundle plus the Tauri shell. It uses the WebView2
  runtime that Windows provides; **that runtime is not part of the installer** and is not covered by
  this file. The installer is built by NSIS, whose runtime stub is inside it.

**STEP support is gone, and with it OpenCascade.** Earlier versions of this application read STEP
files through `occt-import-js` (Open CASCADE Technology, LGPL-2.1). Neither `Cargo.lock` nor
`pnpm-lock.yaml` contains it any more, and neither artifact includes any of that code, so no LGPL
obligation arises from this application.

## Components that are not packages

### The Rust standard library (both artifacts)

Licensed Apache-2.0 OR MIT, Copyright The Rust Project Developers. It is statically linked into every
Rust program. A few of its own dependencies (for example `gimli`, `addr2line`, `object`,
`miniz_oxide`, `hashbrown`, `rustc-demangle`) travel with it under Apache-2.0 OR MIT or Zlib; the
complete list with their licence texts is `share/doc/rust/COPYRIGHT-library.html` in every Rust
toolchain (`rustup` installs it). The Apache-2.0 and MIT texts are reproduced in the Rust crates
section below.

### SQLite (both artifacts)

Compiled from source by `libsqlite3-sys` (feature `bundled`). SQLite is in the public domain; its
source carries this notice in place of a licence:

```text
The author disclaims copyright to this source code.  In place of
a legal notice, here is a blessing:

   * May you do good and not evil.
   * May you find forgiveness for yourself and forgive others.
   * May you share freely, never taking more than you give.
```

### musl libc (`spm-server` Docker image only)

The image's executable is statically linked against musl libc (target `x86_64-unknown-linux-musl`).
musl is licensed under the MIT licence, Copyright 2005-2020 Rich Felker, et al., with a few files
under other permissive licences listed in musl's own `COPYRIGHT` file
(<https://git.musl-libc.org/cgit/musl/tree/COPYRIGHT>), which is the authoritative list; this file
does not reproduce it.


### Microsoft WebView2 loader (desktop only)

The WebView2 loader library is linked statically into the desktop executable by `webview2-com-sys`
(no `WebView2Loader.dll` is installed). It is part of the Microsoft WebView2 SDK, distributed under
the licence below. The `webview2-com-sys` crate does not ship this text; it is reproduced here as it
appears in the SDK's `LICENSE.txt`.

### Source for MPL-2.0 code (desktop only)

The crate `option-ext` 0.2.0 is licensed MPL-2.0 (full text in the Rust licence texts below). It is
used unmodified, and its source is available from <https://crates.io/crates/option-ext/0.2.0>.

### NSIS (desktop installer only)

The installer executable is produced by NSIS (Nullsoft Scriptable Install System), whose runtime stub
and LZMA module are inside it. Below is NSIS's own `COPYING` as installed by the Tauri bundler. The
source code of NSIS is available from <https://nsis.sourceforge.io>.

## Rust crates (223)

The normal dependencies of `spm-server` (Linux, musl: 128 crates) and of the desktop app (Windows: 221 crates), from `Cargo.lock`; 126 are in both. Licences as declared by each crate: MIT OR Apache-2.0 (113), MIT (43), Apache-2.0 OR MIT (21), Unicode-3.0 (15), MIT/Apache-2.0 (5), BSD-3-Clause (3), Unlicense OR MIT (3), Apache-2.0 (2), Unlicense/MIT (2), (MIT OR Apache-2.0) AND Unicode-3.0 (1), 0BSD OR MIT OR Apache-2.0 (1), Apache-2.0 / MIT (1), Apache-2.0 AND MIT (1), Apache-2.0 OR BSL-1.0 (1), BSD-2-Clause OR Apache-2.0 OR MIT (1), BSD-3-Clause AND MIT (1), BSD-3-Clause/MIT (1), CC0-1.0 (1), CC0-1.0 OR MIT-0 OR Apache-2.0 (1), MIT AND BSD-3-Clause (1), MIT OR Apache-2.0 OR Zlib (1), MIT OR Zlib OR Apache-2.0 (1), MPL-2.0 (1), Zlib (1), Zlib OR Apache-2.0 OR MIT (1). Where a crate offers a choice (`MIT OR Apache-2.0`), the texts of every licence file it ships are
given; the recipient may choose any of them. Crates whose texts are identical share one entry.
